From the Hecke Category to the Unipotent Locus
Minh-Tâm Quang Trinh

Abstract
. Let W W be the Weyl group of a split semisimple group G G . Its Hecke category 𝖧 W \mathsf{H}_{W} can be built from pure perverse sheaves on the double flag variety of G G . By developing a formalism of generalized realization functors, we construct a monoidal trace from 𝖧 W \mathsf{H}_{W} to a category of bigraded modules over a certain graded ring: namely, the endomorphisms of the equivariant Springer sheaf over the unipotent locus of G G . We prove that: (1) On objects attached to positive braids β \beta , the output is the weight-graded, equivariant Borel–Moore homology of a generalized Steinberg scheme 𝒵 ⁡ ( β ) \mathcal{Z}(\beta) . (2) Our functor contains, as a summand, one used by Webster–Williamson to construct the Khovanov–Rozansky link invariant. In particular, the Khovanov–Rozansky homology of the link closure of β \beta is fully encoded in the Springer theory of 𝒵 ⁡ ( β ) \mathcal{Z}(\beta) . Decategorifying, we get a trace on the Iwahori–Hecke algebra, valued in graded virtual characters of W W . We give a formula for it in terms of a pairing on characters of W W called Lusztig’s exotic Fourier transform, which generalizes it to all finite Coxeter groups. Using this formula, we establish properties of the trace like rationality, symmetry, and compatibility with parabolic induction. We also show that on periodic braids, the trace produces the characters of explicit virtual modules over Cherendik’s rational double affine Hecke algebra. For W = S n W=S_{n} , we recover an identity of Gorsky–Oblomkov–Rasmussen–Shende relating these modules to the HOMFLY polynomials of torus knots.



Introduction

0.1.
Summary

Let
\mathbf{V}
be a finite-dimensional vector space over a subfield of
\mathbf{R}
, and let
W
be a finite group of automorphisms of
\mathbf{V}
generated by reflections.
Coxeter showed that
W
admits a remarkable presentation in terms of generators and relations
[
Co
]
.
Via this presentation, the group ring of
W
can be deformed to a
\mathbf{Z}[\bm{q}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]
-algebra known as the Iwahori–Hecke algebra
H_{W}
.
One can further define an infinite group
\mathit{Br}_{W}
, generalizing the braid groups studied in geometric topology, such that
W
and
H_{W}
are respective quotients of
\mathit{Br}_{W}
and
\mathbf{Z}[\bm{q}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}][\mathit{Br}_{W}]
.

If
W=S_{n}
, the symmetric group on
n
letters, then
\mathit{Br}_{W}=\mathit{Br}_{n}
, the classical braid group on
n
strands.
Any braid
\beta
can be closed up, end-to-end, to yield a topological link
\hat{\beta}
in
3
-dimensional space.
If
\beta,\beta^{\prime}\in\mathit{Br}_{n}
are conjugate, then
\hat{\beta},\hat{\beta}^{\prime}
are isotopic.
Ocneanu, building on work of Jones, used this observation to construct an isotopy invariant of links called the HOMFLY series out of class functions on the groups
\mathit{Br}_{n}
.
These functions, in turn, factor through functions called Markov traces on the algebras
H_{S_{n}}

[
HOMFLY
,
J
]
.
Y. Gomi discovered that the latter admit a uniform generalization to any finite Coxeter group
W

[
G
]
.

It is now known that
H_{W}
is the (triangulated) Grothendieck ring of a monoidal triangulated category that we will call the Hecke category
\mathsf{H}_{W}
.
Gomi’s Markov trace is categorified by an additive functor

(1)

\displaystyle\mathsf{HHH}:\mathsf{H}_{W}\to\mathsf{Vect}_{3},

where
\mathsf{Vect}_{3}
denotes the category of triply-graded vector spaces.
As
W
runs over the symmetric groups, these functors can be used to construct a link invariant valued in
\mathsf{Vect}_{3}
, originally constructed by Khovanov–Rozansky
[
KR
,
Kh
]
.
More precisely, there is a map from braids
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{W}
to objects
\mathcal{R}(\beta)\in\mathsf{H}_{W}
, due to Rouquier
[
R06
]
, and
\mathsf{HHH}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))
can be used to construct an isotopy invariant of
\hat{\beta}
that decategorifies to its HOMFLY series.

The most general definition of
\mathsf{H}_{W}
is purely algebraic: the homotopy category of Soergel bimodules over
\Sym(\mathbf{V})

[
EW14
,
EW16
]
.
However, in representation theory, we are especially interested in the case where
\mathbf{V}
is the span of a root lattice and
W
is the Weyl group of the corresponding semisimple algebraic group
G
over an algebraically closed field, say,
\bar{\mathbf{F}}
for a finite field
\mathbf{F}
.
Here, the Hecke category can be defined in terms of the geometry of
G
, or rather, its split form
G_{0}
over
\mathbf{F}
.
We are led to hope that
\mathsf{HHH}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))
might be expressed in terms of the (co)homology of an explicit variety over
\mathbf{F}
attached to
\beta
.

In the series of papers
[
WW08
,
WW11
,
WW17
]
, Webster–Williamson relate
\mathsf{HHH}
to the weight grading on the
G
-equivariant cohomology of certain varieties with maps to
G
.
The most inexplicit part of their work is a procedure they call “chromatography,” which turns an object of the mixed derived category
\mathsf{D}_{G,m}^{b}(G_{0})=\mathsf{D}_{G,m}^{b}(G_{0},\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell})
into a complex up to homotopy whose terms are pure complexes of sheaves.
The use of this procedure means that in general, their work only relates
\mathsf{HHH}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))
to a combination of several varieties, rather than to a single one.
These varieties encode the term-by-term structure of
\mathcal{R}(\beta)
, when
\mathcal{R}(\beta)
is viewed as a complex of Soergel bimodules.

One of our goals is to simplify this situation.
Instead of chromatography, we will use a formalism of weight realization functors, inspired by works of Bezrukavnikov–Yun
[
BY
]
, Lusztig–Yun
[
LY20
]
, and Rider
[
R
]
.
For special braids
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{n}
called Richardson braids, Galashin–Lam have recently used Rider’s work to identify a certain summand of
\mathsf{HHH}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))
, its so-called “lowest Hochschild degree,” with the torus-equivariant compactly-supported cohomology of a positroid variety
[
GL
]
.
Their argument generalizes to any Weyl group
W
.
We go further:
For any positive
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{W}
, we realize the entirety of
\mathsf{HHH}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))
within the equivariant Borel–Moore homology of an explicit
G
-variety over the unipotent locus of
G
.

Our viewpoint will lead us to introduce a refinement of
\mathsf{HHH}
, valued in modules rather than vector spaces.
Henceforth, we base-change
\mathbf{V}
to a field of the form
\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}
for some prime
\ell
invertible in
\mathbf{F}
.
Form the semidirect-product ring

(2)

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{W}=\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}[W]\ltimes\Sym(\mathbf{V}),

endowed with the grading where
W
and
\mathbf{V}
are respectively placed in degrees
0
and
2
.
Let
\mathsf{Mod}_{2}^{b}(\mathbf{A}_{W})
be the category of
\mathbf{A}_{W}
-modules equipped with a bigrading where the
\mathbf{A}_{W}
-action is diagonally graded, both gradings are bounded below, and the difference between the gradings is uniformly bounded.
We will construct a contravariant(!) monoidal trace

(3)

\displaystyle\mathsf{AH}:\mathsf{H}_{W}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathsf{Mod}_{2}^{b}(\mathbf{A}_{W}).

We will show that
\mathsf{HHH}^{\vee}
is the sum of the
\Alt^{i}(\mathbf{V})
-isotypic components of
\mathsf{AH}
, where
\Alt^{i}(\mathbf{V})
denotes the
i
th exterior power of
\mathbf{V}
, viewed as a representation of
W
.
The appearance of
\Alt^{i}(\mathbf{V})
is reminiscent of work of Bezrukavnikov–Tolmachov on a monodromic model of
\mathsf{HHH}

[
BT
]
.
For positive
\beta
, we will find that

(4)

\displaystyle\mathsf{AH}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))\simeq\gr_{\ast}^{\bm{w}}\mathrm{H}_{-\ast}^{!,G}(\mathcal{Z}(\beta))

for a certain
G
-scheme
\mathcal{Z}(\beta)
, where on the right-hand side,
\mathrm{H}_{\ast}^{!,G}(-)=\mathrm{H}_{\ast}^{!,G}(-,\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell})
denotes equivariant Borel–Moore homology and
\bm{w}
denotes the weight filtration coming from an
\mathbf{F}
-structure on
\mathcal{Z}(\beta)
.
In the case of the identity
\mathbf{1}\in\mathit{Br}_{W}
, the reduced scheme
\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{1})^{\mathrm{red}}
is the variety Steinberg introduced in
[
St76
]
.
In particular,
\mathsf{AH}(\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{1}))\simeq\mathbf{A}_{W}
by a result of Lusztig
[
L88
]
.

(To explain the contravariance:
The definition of
\mathsf{AH}
will involve a
\Hom
functor.
It turns out that we need a contravariant, not covariant,
\Hom
to get the right-hand side of (
4
) to match
\mathsf{AH}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))
rather than
\mathsf{AH}(\mathcal{R}(\beta^{-1}))
.)

Let us sketch the geometry that underlies
\mathsf{H}_{W}
and
\mathsf{AH}
.
Let
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{0}\otimes_{\mathbf{F}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}
and
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{0}\otimes_{\mathbf{F}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}
denote the flag variety and unipotent locus of
G=G_{0}\otimes_{\mathbf{F}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}
.
The Hecke category of
W
can be interpreted as

(5)

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}_{W}=\mathsf{K}^{b}(\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{B}_{0}\times\mathcal{B}_{0})),

where
\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{B}_{0}\times\mathcal{B}_{0})
is a certain full, additive subcategory of
\mathsf{D}_{G,m}^{b}(\mathcal{B}_{0}\times\mathcal{B}_{0})
generated by pure shifted perverse sheaves.
(More precisely, it is generated by the shift-twists of the intersection complexes of the
G_{0}
-orbits of
\mathcal{B}_{0}\times\mathcal{B}_{0}
.)
The monoidal product on
\mathsf{H}_{W}
arises from a geometrically-defined convolution.
The operation of passing from a braid to its conjugacy class is
very roughly
realized by pulling and pushing sheaves from
\mathcal{B}_{0}\times\mathcal{B}_{0}
to
G_{0}
through the following diagram, implicit in Lusztig’s work on character sheaves and now known as the
horocycle correspondence
:

(6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{0}\times\mathcal{B}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{0}\times\mathcal{B}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{0}}
\mathit{act}
\mathit{pr}

Above,
\mathit{act}
and
\mathit{pr}
are the action and projection maps.

We introduce similar subcategories
\mathsf{C}(G_{0})\subseteq\mathsf{D}_{G,m}^{b}(G_{0})
and
\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{U}_{0})\subseteq\mathsf{D}_{G,m}^{b}(\mathcal{U}_{0})
.
(The category
\mathsf{C}(G_{0})
is generated by objects of the form
E\otimes M
, where
E
is a mixed version of a unipotent character sheaf and
M
is a pure weight-zero Frobenius module; the category
\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{U}_{0})
is generated by the pullbacks of these objects along the inclusion
i:\mathcal{U}_{0}\to G_{0}
.)
In particular,
\mathsf{C}(G_{0})
contains the Grothendieck–Springer sheaf
\mathcal{G}
, while
\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{U}_{0})
contains the Springer sheaf
\mathcal{S}\simeq i^{\ast}\mathcal{G}\langle-{\dim\mathbf{V}}\rangle
.
The horocycle correspondence gives rise to a functor

(7)

\displaystyle\mathsf{CH}:\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{B}_{0}\times\mathcal{B}_{0})\to\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{U}_{0}),

while pullback gives rise to a functor
i^{\ast}\langle-{\dim\mathbf{V}}\rangle:\mathsf{C}(G_{0})\to\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{U}_{0})
.

Crucially,
\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{B}_{0}\times\mathcal{B}_{0})
and
\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{U}_{0})
satisfy a certain
\Ext
-vanishing condition (see Section
1
) that allows us to define realization functors
\rho:\mathsf{K}^{b}(\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{B}_{0}^{2}))\to\mathsf{D}_{G,m}^{b}(\mathcal{B}_{0}^{2})
and
\rho:\mathsf{K}^{b}(\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{U}_{0}))\to\mathsf{D}_{G,m}^{b}(\mathcal{U}_{0})
.
Even though we are unable to construct a similar functor for
G_{0}
, the diagram

(8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{K}^{b}(\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{B}_{0}\times\mathcal{B}_{0}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{K}^{b}(\mathsf{C}(G_{0}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{K}^{b}(\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{U}_{0}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}_{G,m}^{b}(\mathcal{B}_{0}\times\mathcal{B}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}_{G,m}^{b}(G_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}_{G,m}^{b}(\mathcal{U}_{0})}
\mathsf{K}^{b}i^{\ast}\langle-r\rangle
i^{\ast}\langle-r\rangle
\mathsf{K}^{b}\mathsf{CH}
\mathsf{CH}
\rho
\rho

commutes.
The monoidal trace
\mathsf{AH}
is the composition

(9)

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}_{W}=\mathsf{K}^{b}(\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{B}_{0}\times\mathcal{B}_{0}))\to\mathsf{D}_{G,m}^{b}(\mathcal{U}_{0})\xrightarrow{\gr_{\ast}^{\bm{w}}\underline{\Hom}^{\ast}(-,\mathcal{S})}\mathsf{Mod}_{2}^{b}(\mathbf{A}_{W}),

where the first arrow is the functor produced by the diagram, and
\underline{\Hom}^{\ast}
denotes an
\Ext
-space endowed with a Frobenius action.
We use:

(1)

The bottom-left part of the diagram to match
\mathsf{AH}
with the homology of the generalized Steinberg scheme
\mathcal{Z}(\beta)
(in Section
5
).

(2)

The upper-right part of the diagram to match
\Hom_{W}(\Alt^{\ast}(\mathbf{V}),\mathsf{AH})
with
\mathsf{HHH}
(in Section
6
).

The main obstacle with (2) is that Webster–Williamson interpret
\mathsf{HHH}
using the hypercohomology of objects over
G
, whereas we define
\mathsf{AH}
using objects over
\mathcal{U}
.
Moreover, in their work, the Hochschild degree of
\mathsf{HHH}
arises from
\gr_{\ast}^{\bm{w}}
, while in our work, it arises from
\Alt^{\ast}(\mathbf{V})
.
So the matching in (2) is fairly indirect.
Using orthogonality results of Lusztig
[
L85-II
]
and Rider–Russell
[
RR
]
, we reduce the problem to checking certain direct-summand categories
\mathsf{C}_{\hat{W}}(G_{0})\subseteq\mathsf{C}(G_{0})
and
\mathsf{C}_{\hat{W}}(\mathcal{U}_{0})\subseteq\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{U}_{0})
, which are roughly the “principal blocks” generated by
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{S}
.
On the level of these blocks, the key input is a geometrically-defined isomorphism between
\underline{\End}^{\ast}(\mathcal{S})
and the pure part of
\underline{\End}^{\ast}(\mathcal{G})
(in Section
4
).

As for (1), let us sketch the definition of
\mathcal{Z}(\beta)
.
Let
\mathit{Br}_{W}^{+}\subseteq\mathit{Br}_{W}
be the monoid of positive braids.
The map
\mathit{Br}_{W}\to W
admits a set-theoretic section
W\to\mathit{Br}_{W}^{+}
that we will denote
w\mapsto\sigma_{w}
.
(In fact, the images of the
\sigma_{w}
in
H_{W}
form a
\mathbf{Z}[\bm{q}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]
-basis.)
Deligne
[
D97
]
and Broué–Michel
[
BM97
]
introduced a map

(10)

\displaystyle\beta\mapsto O(\beta)

from elements of
\mathit{Br}_{W}^{+}
to
G
-varieties over
\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B}
, such that
O(\sigma_{w})
is the
G
-orbit of
\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B}
indexed by
w
, and such that
O
takes compositions of braids to fiber products over
\mathcal{B}
, up to explicit isomorphisms.
We form a cartesian square:

(11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces O(\beta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}(\beta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{B}}
\mathit{act}

Then the map
\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{1})\to\mathcal{U}
is the Springer resolution.
We define
\mathcal{Z}(\beta)
be the fiber product
\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{1})\times_{\mathcal{U}}\mathcal{U}(\beta)
.

In the rest of the paper, we study the decategorification of
\mathsf{AH}
.
Let
R(W)
be the representation ring of
W
.
Any
M\in\mathsf{Mod}_{2}^{b}(\mathbf{A}_{W})
decategorifies to an element

(12)

\displaystyle[M]_{\bm{q}}=\sum_{i,j}{(-1)^{i-j}}\bm{q}^{\frac{j}{2}}M^{i,j}\in R(W)(\!(\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}})\!)

that we call its graded character.
As a result,
\mathsf{AH}
decategorifies to a
\mathbf{Z}[\bm{q}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]
-linear function
H_{W}\to R(W)(\!(\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}})\!)
.
After multiplying by a normalization factor that clears denominators, we get a function

(13)

\displaystyle\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}:H_{W}\to R(W)[\bm{q}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]

that admits an especially simple formula in terms of the character theory of
H_{W}
.
If we write
\hat{W}
for the set of irreducible characters of
W
, then it is

(14)

\displaystyle\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}=\sum_{\phi,\psi\in\hat{W}}{\{\phi,\psi\}}\phi_{\bm{q}}(\beta)\psi,

where
\phi_{\bm{q}}:H_{W}\to\mathbf{Z}[\bm{q}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]
corresponds to
\phi:\mathbf{Z}[W]\to\mathbf{Z}
under Tits’s deformation theorem and
\{-,-\}
is a
\mathbf{Q}
-valued pairing on
\hat{W}
derived from Lusztig’s work on the character theory of
G(\mathbf{F})

[
L84-1
]
.
The function
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
can be used to recover Gomi’s Markov trace, and our formula is closely related to his formula.
In particular, we will show that various properties of the Markov trace extend to
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
: its rationality, the palindromic symmetry of its coefficients, and its compatibility with parabolic subgroups of
W
.

The function
w\mapsto\langle{\sigma_{w}}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
, evaluated on elements
w\in W
of minimal Bruhat length in their conjugacy classes, recovers the function that Lusztig–Yun introduce in
[
LY19
]
, up to a normalization factor.
Thus it also encodes the map from conjugacy classes in
W
to unipotent classes in
G
that Lusztig constructs in
[
L11
]
.

The definition of
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
can be extended to any finite Coxeter group
W
, as a consequence of
[
L93
,
L94
,
M
]
.
In this generality, we will relate special values of
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
to the cyclotomic block theory of
H_{W}
,
i.e.
, to the representation theory of the ring obtained from
H_{W}
by specializing
\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}
to a root of unity.
To this end, we need a central element
\pi\in\mathit{Br}_{W}^{+}
known as the full twist.
We will say that an element
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{W}
is periodic of slope
\nu=\frac{m}{n}\in\mathbf{Q}
iff

(15)

\displaystyle\beta^{n}=\pi^{m}.

For such
\beta
, we give an explicit formula for
\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
in terms of constants of the form
\mathrm{Deg}_{\phi}(e^{2\pi i\nu})
, where
\mathrm{Deg}_{\phi}(\bm{q})
is the so-called generic or unipotent degree of
\phi

[
GP
, §8.1.8]
.
The constants
\mathrm{Deg}_{\phi}(e^{2\pi i\nu})
are integers that roughly control the sizes of the blocks of
H_{W}(e^{\pi i\nu})=H_{W}|_{\bm{q}^{1/2}=e^{\pi i\nu}}
.

As an application, we relate
[\mathsf{AH}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))]_{\bm{q}}
to the representations of a symplectic reflection algebra
\mathbf{D}_{\nu}^{\mathit{rat}}\supseteq\mathbf{A}_{W}
known as the rational Cherednik algebra or rational double affine Hecke algebra (DAHA).
We can view it as a deformation of
\mathbf{D}_{0}^{\mathit{rat}}=\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}[W]\ltimes\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{V})
that depends on
\nu
, where
\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{V})
is the Weyl algebra of differential operators on
\mathbf{V}
.
Like a semisimple Lie algebra,
\mathbf{D}_{\nu}^{\mathit{rat}}
admits a “category
\mathsf{O}
” of well-behaved modules, except that the simple objects are parametrized by
\hat{W}
.
We respectively write
L_{\nu}(\phi)
and
\Delta_{\nu}(\phi)
for the simple and Verma objects indexed by
\phi\in\hat{W}
.
There is a functor from category
\mathsf{O}=\mathsf{O}_{\nu}
to the category of
H_{W}(e^{\pi i\nu})
-modules, known as the Knizhnik–Zamolodchikov functor, that sends
\Delta_{\nu}(\phi)
to the
H_{W}(e^{\pi i\nu})
-module associated with
\phi_{\bm{q}}
.

Any object
M\in\mathsf{O}_{\nu}
gives rise to a graded character
[M]_{\bm{q}}\in R(W)(\!(\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}})\!)
.
When
\beta
is periodic of slope
\nu
, we can express
[\mathsf{AH}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))]_{\bm{q}}
as an explicit virtual sum of the Verma characters
[\Delta_{\nu}(\phi)]_{\bm{q}}
.
At special values of
\nu
, we can say more.
For instance, at so-called cuspidal values, it simplifies to an explicit
nonnegative
sum of
simple
characters
[L_{\nu}(\phi)]_{\bm{q}}
.

When
W
is a Weyl group, we conjecture that this relation between periodic braids and the rational DAHA is categorified by a
\mathbf{D}_{\nu}^{\mathit{rat}}
-action on a modified version of
\mathsf{AH}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))
.
When
W=S_{n}
, our work recovers a theorem of Gorsky–Oblomkov–Rasmussen–Shende relating the HOMFLY polynomials of torus knots to the characters
[L_{\nu}(1)]_{\bm{q}}
, where
1\in\hat{W}
is the trivial character of
W

[
GORS
]
.

0.2.
Outline and Results

Throughout the paper, we use stacky conventions for (co)homological degree and assume that
\mathbf{F}
has large characteristic.
In what follows, we also assume that
\mathbf{V}^{W}=0
.
We set
r=\dim\mathbf{V}
.

In Section
1
, we review background on
G_{0}
-varieties and their derived categories of
\ell
-adic complexes, with special attention to Deligne’s yoga of weights.
Then we introduce the formalism for realization functors that will serve our purposes.
In particular, we introduce a condition called
\underline{\Hom}
-purity that slightly generalizes the condition called Frobenius invariance in
[
R
]
.

In Section
2
, we give explicit definitions of the categories
\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{B}_{0}\times\mathcal{B}_{0})
,
\mathsf{C}(G_{0})
,
\mathsf{C}_{\hat{W}}(G_{0})
,
\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{U}_{0})
,
\mathsf{C}_{\hat{W}}(\mathcal{U}_{0})
, as well as the functors
\mathsf{CH}
,
i^{\ast}\langle-r\rangle
,
\mathsf{PR}
that relate them.
We also introduce the objects
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{S}
, along with their summands.
We give a thorough exposition of the geometric interpretation of the Hecke category that we were unable to find elsewhere.
The main novelty in this section is a proof that
\mathsf{C}(\mathcal{U}_{0})
is
\underline{\Hom}
-pure, using results from
[
L84-2
,
RR
]
.

In Section
3
, we introduce the ring
\mathbf{A}_{W}
and the monoidal trace
\mathsf{AH}
.

In Section
4
, we first review equivariant Borel–Moore homology, then introduce the Steinberg scheme
\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{U}
and its multiplicative analogue
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\to G
.
Using an inductive argument inspired by an unpublished note of Riche, we establish a comparison between the Borel–Moore homologies of
\mathcal{Z}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
, which leads to the following theorem:

Theorem 1
.

The composition
\bigoplus_{n}\gr_{n}^{\bm{w}}\underline{\End}^{n}(\mathcal{G})\to\underline{\End}^{\ast}(\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{i^{\ast}}\underline{\End}^{\ast}(\mathcal{S})
is an isomorphism of graded
\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}
-algebras.

In Section
5
, we first review the positive braid monoid
\mathit{Br}_{W}^{+}\subseteq\mathit{Br}_{W}
.
Then we review the map
\beta\mapsto O(\beta)
studied by Deligne and Broué–Michel and the map
\beta\mapsto\mathcal{R}(\beta)
introduced by Rouquier.
Using our realization formalism, we rederive the folklore comparison between the variety
O(\beta)
and the complexes
\mathcal{R}(\beta)
,
\mathcal{R}(\beta^{-1})
for positive
\beta
.
Using the work in Section
3
, we prove:

Theorem 2
.

We have

(16)

\displaystyle\mathsf{AH}^{i,j}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))\simeq\gr_{j+2r}^{\bm{w}}\mathrm{H}_{-(i+2r)}^{!,G}(\mathcal{Z}(\beta))

for all
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{W}^{+}
and
i,j\in\mathbf{Z}
.

In Section
6
, we review Khovanov–Rozansky homology and its geometric interpretation in terms of the horocycle correspondence due to Webster–Williamson.
We use the work of Sections
2
,
3
, and
4
to show:

Theorem 3
.

With respect to the grading conventions on
\mathsf{AH}
in Section
3
and on
\mathsf{HHH}
in Section
6
, we have

(17)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{HHH}^{i,i+j,k})^{\vee}\simeq\Hom_{W}(\Alt^{i}(\mathbf{V}),\mathsf{AH}^{j+k,j}(-))

as functors
\mathsf{H}_{W}\to\mathsf{Vect}
for all
i,j,k\in\mathbf{Z}
.

Let
\mathcal{X}(\beta)=\mathcal{X}(\beta)_{0}\otimes\bar{\mathbf{F}}
be the fiber of
\mathcal{U}(\beta)=\mathcal{U}(\beta)_{0}\otimes\bar{\mathbf{F}}
above
1\in\mathcal{U}
.
Using well-known properties of the
W
-action on the Springer resolution, we deduce:

Corollary 4
.

We have

(18)

\displaystyle\mathsf{HHH}^{0,j,k}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))^{\vee}

\displaystyle\simeq\gr_{j+2r}^{\bm{w}}\mathrm{H}_{-(j+k+2r)}^{!,G}(\mathcal{U}(\beta)),

(19)

\displaystyle\mathsf{HHH}^{r,r+j,k}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))^{\vee}

\displaystyle\simeq\gr_{j+2(r-N)}^{\bm{w}}\mathrm{H}_{-(j+k+2(r-N))}^{!,G}(\mathcal{X}(\beta)).

for all
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{W}^{+}
, where
N=\dim\mathcal{B}
.

Combining the corollary with a “Serre duality” result for
\mathsf{HHH}

[
GHMN
]
, we find that for
G=\SL_{n}
and any
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{W}^{+}
, there is a weight-preserving isomorphism between the equivariant Borel–Moore homologies of the varieties
\mathcal{U}(\beta)
and
\mathcal{X}(\beta\pi)
.
We revisit this observation in §
0.3.2
.

In Section
7
, we introduce the function
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}:H_{W}\to R(W)[\bm{q}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]
in terms of the background covered in Appendix
A
.
Its definition can be extended to any finite Coxeter group
W
.
In the case of Weyl groups, we show that
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
is the decategorification of
\mathsf{AH}
up to a normalization factor.
The key ingredient is a multiplicity formula for character sheaves that involves
\{-,-\}
, due to Lusztig
[
L85-III
, Cor. 14.11]

[
L85-V
, Thm. 23.1]
.

Theorem 5
.

If
W
is a Weyl group, and
\mathcal{K}\in\mathsf{H}_{W}
decategorifies to
\beta\in H_{W}
, then

(20)

\displaystyle[\mathsf{AH}^{\vee}(\mathcal{K})]_{\bm{q}}=\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}\cdot[\Sym(\mathbf{V})]_{\bm{q}},

where
(\mathsf{AH}^{\vee})^{i,j}=(\mathsf{AH}^{i,j})^{\vee}
for all
i,j
and
[\Sym(\mathbf{V})]_{\bm{q}}=\sum_{i}{\bm{q}^{i}}\Sym^{i}(\mathbf{V})
.

Next, we explain the precise relationship between
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
and the Markov trace studied by Ocneanu and Gomi:
See Proposition
7.4.3
.
It is most conveniently stated in terms of the class function
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}:\mathit{Br}_{W}\to R(W)(\!(\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}})\!)
defined by

(21)

\displaystyle\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}=(-\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}})^{|\beta|}\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}\cdot\varepsilon[\Sym(\mathbf{V})]_{\bm{q}},

where
|\beta|
is the writhe of
\beta
and
\varepsilon
is the sign character of
W
(see §
A.5
).

Using properties of
\{-,-\}
, we show that certain rationality and symmetry properties of the Markov trace generalize to
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
and
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}
.
For Weyl groups, the strongest form of the rationality property will be Theorem
9
, which we prove in Section
8
.
For general Coxeter groups, the remaining properties are:

Theorem 6
.

For all
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{W}
, we have

(22)

\displaystyle\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}

\displaystyle\in\bm{q}^{-\frac{|\beta|}{2}}R(W)[\bm{q}]\cap R(W)[\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}-\varepsilon\bm{q}^{-\frac{1}{2}}],

(23)

\displaystyle\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}

\displaystyle\in R(W)[\![\bm{q}]\!]\cap R(W)(\bm{q})\cap(\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}})^{|\beta|-r}R(W)[(\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}-\bm{q}^{-\frac{1}{2}})^{\pm 1}].

In particular,
\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
and
\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}
are invariant under
\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mapsto-\varepsilon\bm{q}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and
\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mapsto-\bm{q}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
, respectively.
Moreover,
\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}
has degree
|\beta|-r
as a rational function in
\bm{q}
.

In Section
8
, we again assume that
W
is the Weyl group of
G
.
After reviewing background on Deligne–Lusztig virtual representations, we use their properties to express
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}
in terms of the
\mathbf{F}
-point counts of the fibers of the map
\mathcal{U}(\beta)\to\mathcal{U}
.
Namely, we need results of Deligne–Lusztig–Springer
[
DL
, Thm. 6.9]

[
S76
, Thm. 5.6]
, Kazhdan
[
Kaz
]
, Lusztig
[
L84-1
, Cor. 4.24-4.25]
, and Geck–Lusztig
[
G92
, Prop. 1.3]
.
To state the formula, let
\mathcal{B}_{g}
denote the Springer fiber above
g\in G
, and let

(24)

\displaystyle Q_{g}(w)=\sum_{n}{\tr(wF\mid\mathrm{H}^{2n}(\mathcal{B}_{g},\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}))}

for all
w\in W
.
When
G
is a
special
algebraic group in the sense of Serre
[
Gr
]
, the formula below is a kind of virtual weight series for
[\mathcal{Z}(\beta)/G]
, so it is a corollary of Theorem
5
via
[
HR
, Appendix]
and
[
Jo
, Thm. 4.10]
(see Remark
8.5.2
).
However, not all algebraic groups are special.

Theorem 7
.

For all
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{W}^{+}
, we have

(25)

\displaystyle\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}|_{\bm{q}=q}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{r-|\beta|}}{|G^{F}|}\sum_{u\in\mathcal{U}^{F}}|\mathcal{U}(\beta)_{u}^{F}|Q_{u}

in
\mathbf{Q}\otimes R(W)
, where
q=|\mathbf{F}|
.

For
G=\PGL_{n}
, taking
\varepsilon
-isotypic components on both sides and applying the Serre-duality result of
[
GHMN
]
, or Kálmán’s earlier result
[
Kál
]
, recovers a theorem of Shende–Treumann–Zaslow, matching the “lowest”
a
-degree of the HOMFLY series of the link closure of
\beta
with the stacky point count of
[\mathcal{X}(\beta\pi)/G]

[
STZ
, Thm. 6.34]
.
In fact, Kálmán’s result generalizes to a statement about Gomi’s Markov trace.
For general
G
, this generalization and Theorem
7
together imply
[
L21
, Prop. 2.2]
, which Lusztig attributes to Kawanaka
[
Kaw
]
: See Corollary
8.6.3
.

Recall that a subgroup
W^{\prime}\subseteq W
is parabolic iff it is the stabilizer of a vector in
\mathbf{V}
.
In this case, there is an inclusion of Iwahori–Hecke algebras
H_{W^{\prime}}\subseteq H_{W}
.
Using Theorem
7
and the compatibility of Deligne–Lusztig characters with parabolic induction, we show that
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
is similarly compatible with parabolic induction.

Theorem 8
.

If
W
is a Weyl group of rank
r
and
W^{\prime}\subseteq W
is a parabolic subgroup of rank
r^{\prime}
, then we have commutative diagrams:

(26)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{W^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R(W^{\prime})[\bm{q}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R(W)[\bm{q}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]}
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
\Ind_{W^{\prime}}^{W}
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathit{Br}_{W^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R(W^{\prime})[\![\bm{q}]\!]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathit{Br}_{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R(W)[\![\bm{q}]\!]}
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}
\frac{1}{(1-\bm{q})^{r-r^{\prime}}}\Ind_{W^{\prime}}^{W}
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}

In particular,
\langle{\mathbf{1}}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
is the character of the regular representation of
W
, placed in
\bm{q}
-degree zero, and
\langle{\mathbf{1}}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}=(1-\bm{q})^{-r}\langle{\mathbf{1}}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
.

To conclude Section
8
, we bound the pole of
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}
at
\bm{q}=1
using Theorem
7
and an idea of Lusztig from
[
L11
, §5]
.
For all
w\in W
, let
r(w)
be the dimension of the fixed subspace
\mathbf{V}^{w}\subseteq\mathbf{V}
.

Theorem 9
.

If
W
is a Weyl group and
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{W}^{+}
maps to
w\in W
, then

(27)

\displaystyle\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}\in\frac{1}{(1-\bm{q})^{r(w)}}\,R(W)[\bm{q}].

That is, the pole of
\langle{-}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}
at
\bm{q}=1
is at most
r(w)
.

In the case where
\beta=\sigma_{w}
for some
w\in W
, this result follows from work of Lusztig–Yun in
[
LY19
]
.
Indeed, if
w\in W
has minimal Bruhat length in its conjugacy class, then Theorem
7
essentially implies

(28)

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma_{w}}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}=\frac{\Psi(w)}{(1-\bm{q})^{r(w)}},

where
\Psi:W\to R(W)[\bm{q}]
is the function introduced in
ibid.

In Sections
9
-
10
, we allow
W
to be any finite Coxeter group and
\mathbf{V}
any realization of
W
such that
\mathbf{V}^{W}=0
.
In Section
9
, we compute
\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
for periodic
\beta
.
The key input is that, essentially by Schur’s lemma, the full twist
\pi
acts on any simple
H_{W}
-module by a monomial in
\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}
.
This, together with a deformation argument of Jones
[
J
, §9]
, allows us to determine the character values
\phi_{\bm{q}}(\beta)
whenever some nonzero power of
\beta
equals a power of the full twist.

Theorem 10
.

If
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{W}
is periodic of slope
\nu
, then

(29)

\displaystyle\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}=\sum_{\phi\in\hat{W}}{\bm{q}^{\nu\mathbf{c}(\phi)}}\mathrm{Deg}_{\phi}(e^{2\pi i\nu})\phi,

where
\mathrm{Deg}_{\phi}(\bm{q})\in\mathbf{Q}[\bm{q}]
and
\mathbf{c}(\phi)\in\mathbf{Z}
are the unipotent degree and content of
\phi
, respectively (see §
A.8
, §
A.10
).

In the setting of the above theorem, we will have
\mathrm{Deg}_{\phi}(e^{2\pi i\nu})\in\mathbf{Z}
for all
\phi
and
\nu
.
It will be convenient to set

(30)

\displaystyle\Omega_{\nu}=\bigoplus_{\phi\in\hat{W}}\mathrm{Deg}_{\phi}(e^{2\pi i\nu})\Delta_{\nu}(\phi),

viewed as a virtual
\mathbf{D}_{\nu}^{\mathit{rat}}
-module.
In Section
10
, we deduce:

Corollary 11
.

If
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{W}
is periodic of slope
\nu
, then

(31)

\displaystyle{[\Omega_{\nu}]_{\bm{q}}}=(\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}})^{r-|\beta|}\cdot\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}

in
R(W)[(\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}-\bm{q}^{-\frac{1}{2}})^{\pm 1}]
.

In what follows, let
d_{1},\ldots,d_{r}
be the invariant degrees of the
W
-action on
\mathbf{V}

[
GP
, 149]
.
We say that
\nu\in\mathbf{Q}
is:

(1)

A singular slope iff
\nu\in\frac{1}{d_{i}}\mathbf{Z}
for some
i
.

(2)

A regular slope iff
W
contains a
e^{2\pi i\nu}
-regular element
w
.
This means
w
has an eigenvector in
\mathbf{V}
with eigenvalue
e^{2\pi i\nu}
and trivial
W
-stabilizer
[
S74
]
.

(3)

A regular elliptic slope iff, in (2), we can pick
w
so that
\mathbf{V}^{w}=0
.

(4)

A cuspidal slope iff
\nu\in\frac{1}{d_{i}}\mathbf{Z}
for a unique index
i
.

Conditions (1)-(4) are ordered by increasing strictness.
By work of Broué–Michel
[
BM97
]
, condition (2) is equivalent to the existence of a periodic braid of slope
\nu
.

In Section
10
, we use tools from the representation theory of
\mathbf{D}_{\nu}^{\mathit{rat}}
, especially the Knizhnik–Zamolodchikov functor from category
\mathsf{O}_{\nu}
to the category of
H_{W}(e^{\pi i\nu})
-modules
[
GGOR
]
, to prove:

Theorem 12
.

Let
\nu\in\mathbf{Q}
.

(1)

L_{\nu}(1)
occurs in
\Omega_{\nu}
with multiplicity one.

(2)

If
\nu
is a singular slope, then
\Omega_{\nu}
is a virtual linear combination of torsion modules over the subring
\Sym(\mathbf{V}^{\vee})\subseteq\mathbf{D}_{\nu}^{\mathit{rat}}
.

(3)

If
\nu
is a regular elliptic slope,
\nu>0
, and
W
is a Weyl group, then

(32)

\displaystyle[\Omega_{\nu}]_{\bm{q}}\in R(W)[\bm{q}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}].

That is,
\Omega_{\nu}
is a difference of finite-dimensional
\mathbf{D}_{\nu}^{\mathit{rat}}
-modules.

(4)

If
\nu
is a cuspidal slope and
\nu>0
, then

(33)

\displaystyle[\Omega_{\nu}]_{\bm{q}}=\sum_{\psi\in\hat{W}_{1}}{[L_{\nu}(\psi)]_{\bm{q}}},

where
\hat{W}_{1}\subseteq\hat{W}
is the set of elements of greatest
\mathbf{a}
-value within their
e^{2\pi i\nu}
-blocks, among the
e^{2\pi i\nu}
-blocks of
H_{W}
of defect
1
(see §
A.11
).

Parts (1) and (2) together imply a result of Dunkl–de Jeu–Opdam
[
DJO
, Thm. 4.9(1)]
, while part (3) implies a result of Varagnolo–Vasserot
[
VV
, Thm. 2.8.1(a)]
.
The key machinery in the proof of part (4) is work of Geck on the block theory of
H_{W}
, which shows that when
\nu
is cuspidal, the values
\mathrm{Deg}_{\phi}(e^{2\pi i\nu})
are computable and all belong to
\{-1,0,1\}

[
G92
]
.
Part (4) implies:

Corollary 13
.

Keeping the hypotheses of Theorem
12
, suppose that
W
is irreducible (see §
A.1
) and at least one of the following is true:

•

W
is not of type
E_{8}
or
H_{4}
.

•

\nu\notin\frac{1}{15}\mathbf{Z}
.

Then
[\Omega_{\nu}]_{\bm{q}}=[L_{\nu}(1)]_{\bm{q}}
.

For
W=S_{n}
, Theorem
3
, Corollary
11
, and Corollary
13
together recover
[
GORS
, Thm. 1.1]
, which was originally proved by explicit calculation in the ring of symmetric functions.

In Appendix
A
, we review facts about the structure and representation theory of
W
and
H_{W}
that we need elsewhere, including the definitions of the pairing
\{-,-\}
and the unipotent degrees
\mathrm{Deg}_{\phi}
.

In Appendix
B
, we state precise comparisons between the schemes
\mathcal{U}(\beta)
,
\mathcal{Z}(\beta)
,
\mathcal{X}(\beta)
and the varieties in the works of Lusztig
[
L11
]
, Shende–Treumann–Zaslow
[
STZ
]
, Mellit
[
Me
]
, Casals–Gorsky–Gorsky–Simental
[
CGGS
]
, and others.

In Appendix
C
, we list values for the characters
\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}
and
\langle{\beta}\rangle_{\bm{q}}^{0}
in low rank, as well as an example where
\beta
is an iterated torus braid.

0.3.
Conjectures and Questions

0.3.1.

The appearance of the ring
\mathbf{A}_{W}
suggests that
\mathsf{AH}
is closely related to a functor on
\mathsf{H}_{W}
called the horizontal trace, which
[
QRS
,
GW
,
GHW
]
studied in the case where
W=S_{n}
via diagrammatic rather than geometric methods.
If
\star
denotes the monoidal product on
\mathsf{H}_{W}
, then the horizontal trace is roughly the universal additive functor under which
\mathcal{K}\star\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}\star\mathcal{K}
become isomorphic for all objects
\mathcal{K},\mathcal{L}\in\mathsf{H}_{W}
.

In
[
QRS
]
, Queffelec–Rose–Sartori show that the horizontal trace refines
\mathsf{HHH}
, and in
[
GW
]
, Gorsky–Wedrich roughly show that it takes values in the homotopy category of
\mathbf{A}_{W}
-modules.
(The difference is that Gorsky–Wedrich take
\mathbf{V}
to be the
n
-dimensional permutation representation of
S_{n}
, rather than its
(n-1)
-dimensional irreducible summand.)

Conjecture 14
.

After we replace the semisimple group
G
with the reductive group
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
, our procedure to construct
\mathsf{AH}
yields (the objectwise dual of) (the homology of) the horizontal trace studied in
[
GW
]
.

0.3.2.

We expect the geometry of
\mathcal{U}(\beta)
to manifest a “Serre duality” phenomenon involving the full twist.
Gorsky–Hogancamp–Mellit–Nakagane showed that for
G=\SL_{n}
and any
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{W}
, the lowest Hochschild degree of
\mathsf{HHH}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))
and the highest Hochschild degree of
\mathsf{HHH}(\mathcal{R}(\beta\pi))
coincide
[
GHMN
]
, which categorifies a theorem of Kálmán about the HOMFLY series
[
Kál
]
.
Corollary
4
leads us to expect a geometric explanation of this coincidence.

In earlier versions of this work, we hoped for the existence of an algebraic homeomorphism from
[\mathcal{X}(\beta\pi)/G]
onto
[\mathcal{U}(\beta)/G]
that would explain the weight-preserving isomorphism between their Borel–Moore homologies.
Unfortunately, as we will explain in future work, this conjecture already fails for
G=\SL_{3}
.

Question 15
.

Is there a geometric explanation for the (shifted) isomorphism
\gr_{\ast}^{\bm{w}}\mathrm{H}_{\ast}^{!,G}(\mathcal{X}(\beta\pi))\simeq\gr_{\ast}^{\bm{w}}\mathrm{H}_{\ast}^{!,G}(\mathcal{U}(\beta))
?

0.3.3.

Oblomkov–Rozansky have recently proved that the Khovanov–Rozansky invariant of a
knot
enjoys a bigraded symmetry that categorifies the
\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mapsto-\bm{q}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
symmetry of its HOMFLY series
[
OR
]
, resolving a conjecture from
[
DGR
]
.
We expect this result to extend to
\mathsf{AH}
.
More precisely, we expect that for certain positive braids
\beta
, the stack
[\mathcal{Z}(\beta)/G]
enjoys a “curious Lefschetz” property that implies a corresponding bigraded symmetry of the module
\mathsf{AH}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))
, by way of Theorem
2
.

Following Hausel–Rodriguez-Villegas
[
HR
]
, if
X_{0}
is (a stack quotient of) a variety over
\mathbf{F}
of dimension
d
and
X=X_{0}\otimes\bar{\mathbf{F}}
, then we say that the curious Lefschetz property holds for
X
iff there are isomorphisms

(34)

\displaystyle\gr_{d-j}^{\bm{w}}\mathrm{H}_{!}^{i}(X,\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell})\xrightarrow{\sim}\gr_{d+j}^{\bm{w}}\mathrm{H}_{!}^{i+j}(X,\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell})

of compactly-supported cohomology for all
i
and
j
, induced by the cup product with a fixed class in
\mathrm{H}_{!}^{2}
.
This means the series

(35)

\displaystyle(\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}})^{-d}\sum_{i,j}\bm{q}^{\frac{j}{2}}t^{i}\gr_{j}^{\bm{w}}\mathrm{H}_{!}^{i}(X,\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell})

is invariant under
\bm{q}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mapsto\bm{q}^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{-1}
.
Mellit has recently established this property for character varieties with structure group
G=\GL_{n}

[
Me
]
.
In the course of his proof, he also establishes the property for certain varieties closely related to the fibers of the maps
\mathcal{X}(\beta)\to\mathcal{B}
(see Appendix
B
).
His results, as well as our Theorem
6
, suggest evidence for:

Conjecture 16
.

For all
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{W}^{+}
, the curious Lefschetz property holds for
\mathcal{U}(\beta)
and
\mathcal{Z}(\beta)
.
If, in addition,
\beta
maps to an element
w\in W
such that
\mathbf{V}^{w}=0
, then the property also holds for their stack quotients by
G
.

Note that for
W=S_{n}
, the hypothesis that
w
satsfies
\mathbf{V}^{w}=0
is equivalent to the hypothesis that the link closure of
\beta
is a knot.

In
[
GH
]
, Gorsky–Hogancamp introduce a refinement of
\mathsf{HHH}
called
y
-ified homology
and denoted
\mathsf{HY}
.
They conjecture that the bigraded symmetry of the Khovanov–Rozansky invariant can be extended from knots to arbitrary links by replacing
\mathsf{HHH}
with
\mathsf{HY}
.
In future work, we will relate
\mathsf{HY}
to a
monodromic
refinement of
\mathsf{AH}
.

0.3.4.

Lastly, we propose a (partial) categorification of Theorem
10
, which would strengthen some of the conjectures from
[
GORS
]
relating knot invariants and the rational DAHA.

Let
W=S_{n}
and
\nu=\frac{m}{n}
, with
m,n>0
coprime.
Gorsky, Oblomkov, Rasmussen, and Shende construct three filtrations on the simple
\mathbf{D}_{\nu}^{\mathit{rat}}
-module
L_{\nu}(1)
that are compatible with the grading on its character.
They conjecture that all three are the same filtration
\bm{f}_{\leq\ast}
, and that their theorem can be promoted to an isomorphism between the Khovanov–Rozansky homology of the
(m,n)
torus knot and the sum of the
\Alt^{i}(\mathbf{V})
-isotypics of
\gr_{\ast}^{\bm{f}}L_{\nu}(1)
.
One of the candidate filtrations is constructed geometrically, as we now explain.

For any almost-simple, simply-connected group
G
and regular elliptic slope
\nu
, Oblomkov–Yun have related the DAHA module
L_{\nu}(1)
to a complex projective
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-variety that we will denote
\mathcal{B}_{\nu}^{\mathit{aff}}

[
OY
]
.
In technical terms,
\mathcal{B}_{\nu}^{\mathit{aff}}
is an affine Springer fiber of Iwahori type with structure group
G
.
By means of the topology of a generalized Hitchin fibration, its equivariant cohomology is endowed with an increasing filtration
\bm{p}_{\leq\ast}
called the perverse filtration.

There is a flat deformation of
\mathbf{A}_{W}
over
\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}[\epsilon]
that we will denote
\mathbf{A}_{W,\epsilon}
, known as Lusztig’s graded or degenerate affine Hecke algebra and satisfying
\mathbf{A}_{W,\epsilon}|_{\epsilon=0}=\mathbf{A}_{W}
.
Oblomkov–Yun construct an
\mathbf{A}_{W,\epsilon}
-action on
\mathrm{H}_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}^{\ast}(\mathcal{B}_{\nu}^{\mathit{aff}},\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell})
that sends
\epsilon
to the equivariant parameter.
Similarly, there is a flat deformation of
\mathbf{D}_{\nu}^{\mathit{rat}}
over
\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}[\epsilon]
to a graded algebra that we will denote
\mathbf{D}_{\nu,\epsilon}^{\mathit{rat}}
, satisfying
\mathbf{D}_{\nu,\epsilon}^{\mathit{rat}}|_{\epsilon=1}=\mathbf{D}_{\nu}^{\mathit{rat}}

[
OY
, §4.2.4]
.
The
\mathbf{A}_{W,\epsilon}
-action on
\mathrm{H}_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}^{\ast}(\mathcal{B}_{\nu}^{\mathit{aff}},\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell})
induces an
\mathbf{A}_{W}
-action that extends to a
\mathbf{D}_{\nu,\epsilon}^{\mathit{rat}}
-action.
For
G=\SL_{n}
and regular elliptic
\nu
, it turns out that

(36)

\displaystyle\gr_{\ast}^{\bm{p}}\mathrm{H}_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}^{\ast}(\mathcal{B}_{\nu}^{\mathit{aff}},\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell})|_{\epsilon=1}\simeq L_{\nu}(1)

as
\mathbf{D}_{\nu}^{\mathit{rat}}
-modules, via an isomorphism that sends the
\bm{p}
-grading to the grading on
[L_{\nu}(1)]_{\bm{q}}
.
The authors of
[
GORS
]
define the geometric candidate for
\bm{f}_{\leq\ast}
to be the difference of the perverse and cohomological filtrations on the left-hand side of (
36
), up to a shift.

In forthcoming work, we will study a conjectural
transcendental
retraction from the stack
[\mathcal{Z}(\beta)/G]
to the variety
\mathcal{B}_{\nu}^{\mathit{aff}}
for any periodic braid
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{W}^{+}
of regular elliptic slope
\nu
.
It is expected to be a kind of nonabelian Hodge correspondence, where
[\mathcal{Z}(\beta)/G]
plays the role of the “Betti” side and
\mathcal{B}_{\nu}^{\mathit{aff}}
plays the role of the “Dolbeault” side.
In particular, the retraction should transport the doubled weight filtration
\bm{w}_{\leq 2\ast}
on the compactly-supported cohomology of
[\mathcal{Z}(\beta)/G]
to the perverse filtration
\bm{p}_{\leq\ast}
on the cohomology of
\mathcal{B}_{\nu}^{\mathit{aff}}
, up to a degree shift.
We are thus led to conjecture a Betti origin for the filtration
\bm{f}_{\leq\ast}
.

As evidence, note that the
G
-action on
\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{1})
extends to a
(G\times\mathbf{G}_{m})
-action.
The isomorphism
\mathbf{A}_{W}\simeq\mathrm{H}_{-\ast}^{!,G}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{1}))
can be lifted to an isomorphism

(37)

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{W,\epsilon}\simeq\mathrm{H}_{-\ast}^{!,G\times\mathbf{G}_{m}}(\mathcal{Z}(\mathbf{1})),

as shown in
[
L88
]
.
Unfortunately, as Zhiwei Yun pointed out to me, there is no obvious
(G\times\mathbf{G}_{m})
-action on
\mathcal{Z}(\beta)
for other
\beta
, even when
\beta
is periodic.
Even so, I hope for the following:

Conjecture 17
.

There is a flat deformation of
\mathsf{AH}
over
\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{\ell}[\epsilon]
to a functor

(38)

\displaystyle\mathsf{AH}_{\epsilon}:\mathsf{H}_{W}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathsf{Mod}_{2}^{b}(\mathbf{A}_{W,\epsilon})

such that for all
\mathcal{K}\in\mathsf{H}_{W}
:

(1)

The
\mathbf{A}_{W}
-action on
\mathsf{AH}(\mathcal{K})
extends to a
\bm{w}
-
filtered

\mathbf{A}_{W,\epsilon}
-action on
\mathsf{AH}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{K})
, where
\bm{w}
denotes the second grading.
By
[
OY
, Prop. 4.3.1]
, the latter action induces a
\bm{w}
-
graded

\mathbf{A}_{W}
-action on
\mathsf{AH}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{K})
.

If
\beta\in\mathit{Br}_{W}^{+}
is periodic of regular elliptic slope
\nu
, then:

(2)

The
\mathbf{A}_{W}
-action on
\mathsf{AH}_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))
extends to a
\mathbf{D}_{\nu,\epsilon}^{\mathit{rat}}
-action.

(3)

The
\mathbf{D}_{\nu}^{\mathit{rat}}
-module formed by
\mathsf{AH}_{1}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))
contains
L_{\nu}(1)
as a summand with multiplicity one.

(4)

The filtration
\bm{f}_{\leq\ast}
on
L_{\nu}(1)
defined in
[
GORS
]
arises from the difference of the two gradings on
\mathsf{AH}_{1}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))
.

Note that in part (1), the differences between the various specializations of
\epsilon
would still vanish at the level of graded characters:
Thus,
[\mathsf{AH}_{1}^{\vee}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))]_{\bm{q}}
would remain the same as
[\mathsf{AH}^{\vee}(\mathcal{R}(\beta))]_{\bm{q}}
.

Question 18
.

Is there a
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-action on the stack
[\mathcal{Z}(\beta)/G]
that could give rise to the flat deformation of
\mathsf{AH}
in Conjecture
17
?
If not, does there still exist a non-algebraic
\mathbf{C}^{\times}
-action in the complex setting?

0.4.
